## GHSA-2v6v-25fm-p4fg · github.com/seaweedfs/seaweedfs

2026-09-02 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The SeaweedFS filer's IAM gRPC service endpoints, which manage S3 users and access keys, lacked any authentication mechanism. This allowed any unauthenticated client to invoke administrative functions.

### Impact

An attacker could create, modify, or delete S3 users and their access keys, effectively gaining full administrative control over the S3-compatible storage and potentially accessing or manipulating all stored data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/weed/server/filer_server_handlers_iam_grpc.go
+++ b/weed/server/filer_server_handlers_iam_grpc.go
@@ -32,6 +32,30 @@
 
 func NewIamGrpcServer(credentialManager *credential.CredentialManager) *IamGrpcServer {
 	return &IamGrpcServer{
 		credentialManager: credentialManager,
+		adminSigningKey:   adminSigningKey,
 	}
 }
 
+func (s *IamGrpcServer) checkAdminAuth(ctx context.Context) error {
+	if len(s.adminSigningKey) == 0 {
+		return status.Error(codes.PermissionDenied, "iam admin auth not configured")
+	}
+	md, ok := metadata.FromIncomingContext(ctx)
+	if !ok {
+		return status.Error(codes.Unauthenticated, "missing metadata")
+	}
+	authHeaders := md.Get("authorization")
+	if len(authHeaders) == 0 {
+		return status.Error(codes.Unauthenticated, "missing authorization metadata")
+	}
+	raw := strings.TrimSpace(authHeaders[0])
+	parts := strings.Fields(raw)
+	if len(parts) != 2 || !strings.EqualFold(parts[0], "Bearer") || parts[1] == "" {
+		return status.Error(codes.Unauthenticated, "authorization header must use Bearer scheme")
+	}
+	token := parts[1]
+	parsed, err := security.DecodeJwt(s.adminSigningKey, security.EncodedJwt(token), &security.SeaweedFilerAdminClaims{})
+	if err != nil || parsed == nil || !parsed.Valid {
+		return status.Error(codes.Unauthenticated, "invalid admin token")
+	}
+	return nil
+}
+
 //////////////////////////////////////////////////
 // Configuration Management
 
 func (s *IamGrpcServer) GetConfiguration(ctx context.Context, req *iam_pb.GetConfigurationRequest) (*iam_pb.GetConfigurationResponse, error) {
+	if err := s.checkAdminAuth(ctx); err != nil {
+		return nil, err
+	}
+	if req == nil {
+		return nil, status.Errorf(codes.InvalidArgument, "request is required")
+	}
 	glog.V(4).Infof("GetConfiguration")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `checkAdminAuth` method that verifies a Bearer token signed by a pre-configured filer write-signing key. This method is now called at the beginning of every IAM gRPC service handler to ensure only authenticated and authorized requests are processed.

## GHSA-qh9r-j7rp-4x2m · github.com/openchoreo/openchoreo

2026-09-02 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The OpenChoreo Cluster Gateway exposed sensitive management APIs, intended for internal cluster use, on the same public port as the agent WebSocket endpoint. This allowed unauthenticated external attackers to access and invoke these internal data-plane operations.

### Impact

An attacker could perform unauthenticated data-plane operations, potentially leading to full compromise of the data plane, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/cluster-gateway/server.go
+++ b/internal/cluster-gateway/server.go
 	publicMux := http.NewServeMux()
 	publicMux.HandleFunc("/ws", s.handleWebSocket)
 
 	// Internal listener: caller-facing /api/* for in-cluster components only.
 	internalMux := http.NewServeMux()
 	internalMux.HandleFunc("/api/proxy/", s.handleHTTPProxy) // HTTP proxy to data plane services
 
 	// Register plane lifecycle API (for controller notifications and status queries)
 	planeAPI := NewPlaneAPI(s.connMgr, s, s.logger)
-	planeAPI.RegisterRoutes(mux)
+	planeAPI.RegisterRoutes(internalMux)
```

### Fix summary

The patch separates the Cluster Gateway's public-facing agent WebSocket endpoint from its internal management APIs. It introduces a new 'internal-port' for the management APIs, ensuring they are only accessible from within the cluster, while the public port only serves the agent WebSocket.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 58 occurrences**

## GHSA-52gf-6rpq-fgmx · github.com/openchoreo/openchoreo

2026-09-02 · Go · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed users to specify a 'project' parameter when making requests to the 'exec' and 'wirelogs' endpoints. However, the authorization checks were performed against this user-supplied 'project' value, rather than the actual owning project of the targeted component. This allowed an attacker to bypass authorization by simply providing a project they had access to, even if the component belonged to a different, unauthorized project.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands within components belonging to other projects and view wirelogs (sensitive log data) from components in projects they were not authorized to access. This leads to cross-project command execution and sensitive information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/openchoreo-api/api/handlers/exec.go
+++ b/internal/openchoreo-api/api/handlers/exec.go
@@ -66,7 +66,7 @@ func (h *ExecHandler) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
 	componentName := parts[1]
 
 	query := r.URL.Query()
-	project := query.Get("project")
+	requestedProject := query.Get("project")
 	envName := query.Get("env")
 	container := query.Get("container")
 	podName := query.Get("pod")
@@ -78,6 +78,35 @@ func (h *ExecHandler) ServeHTTP(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
 	logger := h.logger.With("namespace", namespace, "component", componentName)
 	logger.Info("Exec request received", "env", envName, "pod", podName, "container", container)
 
+	// Authorize: check that the caller has component:exec permission for this environment.
+	if h.authzChecker == nil {
+		logger.Error("Authorization checker not configured")
+		http.Error(w, "authorization not configured", http.StatusInternalServerError)
+		return
+	}
+
+	// Pin authorization and pod resolution to the component's real owning project
+	// rather than the caller-supplied `project`.
+	project, err := h.resolveComponentProject(ctx, namespace, componentName)
+	if err != nil {
+		status := http.StatusBadRequest
+		var infraErr *execInfraError
+		if errors.As(err, &infraErr) {
+			status = http.StatusServiceUnavailable
+		}
+		logger.Warn("Failed to resolve component for exec", "error", err)
+		http.Error(w, fmt.Sprintf("failed to resolve component: %v", err), status)
+		return
+	}
+	// Fail closed if the caller named a project that does not own the component.
+	// The generic forbidden message avoids disclosing the component's real owner.
+	if requestedProject != "" && requestedProject != project {
+		logger.Warn("requested project does not own the target component; denying exec",
+			"requestedProject", requestedProject, "ownerProject", project)
+		http.Error(w, "you do not have permission to exec into this component", http.StatusForbidden)
+		return
+	}
+
 	// Resolve the target environment before authorizing so per-environment exec
 	// conditions are evaluated against it (`env` may be omitted by the client).
 	effectiveEnv, err := h.resolveEnvName(ctx, namespace, project, envName)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to resolve the true owning project of a component based on its internal configuration, regardless of the 'project' parameter supplied by the user. Authorization checks are now performed against this resolved owning project. Additionally, if the user-supplied 'project' does not match the component's actual owner, the request is explicitly denied to prevent project-spoofing.

## GHSA-g2fm-8hr4-j82h · easycorp/easyadmin-bundle

2026-09-02 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The EasyAdmin custom-action dispatcher swapped the controller for a custom action *after* Symfony's security firewall had already evaluated 'access_control' rules against the original admin URL. This meant that any path-based access_control rule protecting the *target* route of the custom action was silently skipped.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass `access_control` rules defined for specific routes, potentially gaining unauthorized access to sensitive functionality or data that should have been protected.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/EventListener/AdminRouterSubscriber.php
+++ b/src/EventListener/AdminRouterSubscriber.php
@@ -202,6 +208,8 @@ private function getSymfonyControllerFqcn(Request $request): ?string
 
         $newRequest->server->set('REQUEST_URI', $url);
 
+        $this->assertTargetRouteAccessIsGranted($newRequest);
+
         $parameters = $this->requestMatcher->matchRequest($newRequest);
 
         return $parameters['_controller'] ?? null;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `assertTargetRouteAccessIsGranted` method that re-evaluates the access control rules against the target route of the custom action. It uses Symfony's `AccessMapInterface` and `AuthorizationCheckerInterface` to ensure that the user is granted access to the target route before proceeding, effectively mirroring Symfony's own access listener.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 99 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Ruby, JavaScript, Python, PHP, Java, RUST, Erlang, C#

## GHSA-gxmj-r5rf-ggwq · Studio-42/elFinder

2026-09-02 · PHP · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete fix for a previous ZIP extraction bypass. The application's MIME type filtering, intended to prevent malicious file uploads, could be circumvented when extracting ZIP archives. Specifically, a crafted ZIP file could contain PHP files that would be extracted and saved, bypassing the 'uploadDeny' filter.

### Impact

An attacker could upload arbitrary PHP files to the server, leading to remote code execution and full control over the affected system.

### Key diff

```
--- Version 2.1.69 (2.1-src Nightly: 30b2dcac2) (2026-08-03)
+++ Version 2.1.69 (2.1-src Nightly: 4f0c06c3c) (2026-08-03)
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff is for 'elfinder.min.js', which is a minified JavaScript file. It primarily shows a version bump, indicating a new release. The actual fix for the ZIP extraction bypass would be in the backend PHP code, which is not visible in this diff. The JavaScript change itself does not directly address the RCE vulnerability.

## GHSA-qxc2-j82w-r537 · @faker-js/faker

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed in the `resolveProperty` function, which was intended to safely resolve properties. However, when the `entrypoint` was a string, it directly used bracket notation (`entrypoint?.[key as keyof typeof entrypoint]`) to access properties. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript code via the `key` parameter, leading to code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the system where the Faker library is used, potentially leading to full system compromise or data exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/modules/helpers/eval.ts
+++ b/src/modules/helpers/eval.ts
@@ -217,7 +217,7 @@ function resolveProperty(entrypoint: unknown, key: string): unknown {
         return undefined;
       }
 
-      return entrypoint?.[key as keyof typeof entrypoint];
+      return resolveProperty(entrypoint, key);
     }
 
     case 'object': {
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the `resolveProperty` function's behavior when `entrypoint` is a string. Instead of directly accessing properties using bracket notation, it now recursively calls `resolveProperty` with the `entrypoint` and `key`. This prevents direct evaluation of attacker-controlled strings as property accessors.

## GHSA-275h-v5h9-vr82 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-02 · Go · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed an authenticated user to construct a file path using user-controlled input without proper validation. This enabled the user to escape the intended `snippets` directory and access files located elsewhere on the file system.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could read sensitive files outside the intended directory, such as configuration files containing secrets (`conf/conf.json`) or the application's database file (`siyuan.db`), leading to information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/kernel/server/serve.go
+++ b/kernel/server/serve.go
@@ -418,6 +418,18 @@ func serveSnippets(ginServer *gin.Engine) {
 
 		// 没有在配置文件中命中时在文件系统上查找
 		filePath = filepath.Join(util.SnippetsPath, filePath)
+
+		// 限制只能访问 snippets 目录内的文件，并拦截敏感路径，避免通过路径穿越读取工作空间内的敏感文件
+		if !gulu.File.IsSubPath(util.SnippetsPath, filePath) {
+			c.Status(http.StatusUnauthorized)
+			return
+		}
+		if util.IsSensitivePath(filePath) {
+			logging.LogErrorf("refuse to serve sensitive snippet file [%s]", c.Request.URL.Path)
+			c.Status(http.StatusForbidden)
+			return
+		}
+
 		c.File(filePath)
 	})
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces two new checks: `gulu.File.IsSubPath` to ensure the requested file path remains within the `util.SnippetsPath` directory, and `util.IsSensitivePath` to explicitly block access to known sensitive files. If either check fails, the request is denied with an appropriate HTTP status code.

## GHSA-5jgf-p345-68v8 · fast-uri

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The `fast-uri` library failed to consistently apply Internationalized Domain Name (IDN) canonicalization, specifically Punycode conversion, to scheme-relative URI references. This inconsistency meant that a host in a relative URI might not be converted to its ASCII form, leading to a mismatch when compared or resolved against a base URI where the host was correctly canonicalized.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious URI that, when resolved, points to a different host than intended. This could bypass security checks, leading to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF), open redirects, or other host-based vulnerabilities, allowing access to internal resources or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/index.js
+++ b/index.js
@@ -34,6 +34,18 @@ function resolve (baseURI, relativeURI, options) {
   } = parseWithStatus(relativeURI, schemelessOptions)
   if (
     baseMalformed ||
-    relativeMalformed
+    relativeMalformed ||
+    baseMalformedHost ||
+    relativeMalformedHost
   ) {
     throw new Error(baseParsed.error || relativeParsed.error || 'URI is malformed.')
   }
   const resolved = resolveComponents(baseParsed, relativeParsed, schemelessOptions, true)
+  const resolvedSchemeHandler = SCHEMES[((options && options.scheme) || resolved.scheme || '').toLowerCase()]
+  const resolvedHost = resolved.host
+  const resolvedHostIsIP = resolvedHost !== undefined && resolvedHost !== '' &&
+    (normalizeIPv4(resolvedHost).isIPV4 || normalizeIPv6(resolvedHost).isIPV6)
+  canonicalizeHost(resolved, options || {}, resolvedSchemeHandler, resolvedHostIsIP)
+  if (resolved.error && !encodedASCIIHost) {
+    throw new Error(resolved.error)
+  }
   return serialize(resolved, { ...schemelessOptions, skipEscape: true })
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `canonicalizeHost` function that is explicitly called during URI resolution and parsing. This ensures that IDN canonicalization is consistently applied to the host component of both base and relative URIs, and also for the resolved URI, preventing host confusion. It also adds `malformedHost` status to track and reject URIs where host conversion fails.

> → GHSA-7rqj-j65f-68wh
> → GHSA-mwr4-5g34-j5cq

## GHSA-6m44-fpc8-c3rq · mistune

2026-09-02 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The markdown parser did not limit the depth of nested emphasis markers (e.g., `**bold**` or `*italic*`). An attacker could craft an input with excessively nested emphasis, causing the parser to enter a deep recursion during processing, leading to a `RecursionError` and crashing the application.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted markdown input to a service using `mistune`, causing the service to crash and become unavailable, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/mistune/inline_parser.py
+++ b/src/mistune/inline_parser.py
@@ -688,9 +702,12 @@ def _process_emphasis_delimiters(parts: List[Dict[str, Any]], delimiters: List[_
             closer_pos += 1
             continue
 
+        children = parts[opener.index + 1 : closer.index]
+        if max_depth > 0 and _emphasis_depth(children) >= max_depth:
+            closer_pos += 1
+            continue
         opener_text["raw"] = opener_text["raw"][:-use_length]
         closer_text["raw"] = closer_text["raw"][use_length:]
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `max_emphasis_depth` parameter to the `InlineParser` and `_finalize_emphasis_tokens` function, defaulting to 20. It also adds a new helper function `_emphasis_depth` to calculate the current nesting level. The `_process_emphasis_delimiters` function now checks this depth and skips processing further nested emphasis if the maximum depth is exceeded.

## GHSA-f65p-4m7j-42xc · fast-uri

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `fast-uri` library's IPv6 normalization logic was flawed, specifically in how it handled malformed IPv6 addresses and zone identifiers. It incorrectly parsed certain IPv6 formats, allowing an attacker to bypass validation and potentially craft URIs that would resolve to unintended internal resources.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a URI with a specially malformed IPv6 address that, when processed by the vulnerable library, would lead to a Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF). This could allow the attacker to make requests to internal network resources or services that would otherwise be inaccessible.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/utils.js
+++ b/lib/utils.js
-function getIPV6 (input) {
-  let tokenCount = 0
-  const output = { error: false, address: '', zone: '' }
-  /** @type {Array<string>} */
-  const address = []
-  /** @type {Array<string>} */
-  const buffer = []
-  let endipv6Encountered = false
-  let endIpv6 = false
-
-  let consume = consumeHextets
-
-  for (let i = 0; i < input.length; i++) {
-    const cursor = input[i]
-    if (cursor === '[' || cursor === ']') { continue }
-    if (cursor === ':') {
-      if (endipv6Encountered === true) {
-        endIpv6 = true
-      }
-      if (!consume(buffer, address, output)) { break }
-      if (++tokenCount > 7) {
-        // not valid
-        output.error = true
-        break
-      }
-      if (i > 0 && input[i - 1] === ':') {
-        endipv6Encountered = true
-      }
-      address.push(':')
-      continue
-    } else if (cursor === '%') {
-      if (!consume(buffer, address, output)) { break }
-      // switch to zone detection
-      consume = consumeIsZone
-    } else {
-      buffer.push(cursor)
-      continue
-    }
-  }
-  if (buffer.length) {
-    if (consume === consumeIsZone) {
-      output.zone = buffer.join('')
-    } else if (endIpv6) {
-      address.push(buffer.join(''))
-    } else {
-      address.push(stringArrayToHexStripped(buffer))
-    }
-  }
-  output.address = address.join('')
-  return output
+}
+function normalizeIPv6Address (input) {
+  const compression = input.indexOf('::')
+  if (compression !== -1 && input.indexOf('::', compression + 1) !== -1) return undefined
+
+  const left = compression === -1 ? input.split(':') : input.slice(0, compression).split(':')
+  const right = compression === -1 ? [] : input.slice(compression + 2).split(':')
+  if (compression !== -1) {
+    if (left.length === 1 && left[0] === '') left.length = 0
+    if (right.length === 1 && right[0] === '') right.length = 0
+  }
+
+  const parts = left.concat(right)
+  let hextetCount = 0
+  for (let i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {
+    const part = parts[i]
+    if (part === '') return undefined
+
+    if (part.indexOf('.') !== -1) {
+      if (i !== parts.length - 1 || (compression !== -1 && right.length === 0) || !isIPv4(part)) return undefined
+      hextetCount += 2
+      continue
+    }
+
+    if (!isHextet(part)) return undefined
+    parts[i] = parseInt(part, 16).toString(16)
+    hextetCount++
+  }
+
+  if (compression === -1) {
+    if (hextetCount !== 8) return undefined
+    return compressIPv6ZeroRun(parts)
+  }
+  if (hextetCount >= 8) return undefined
+
+  // expand "::" then re-compress the longest run for a canonical result
+  const expanded = parts.slice(0, left.length)
+  for (let i = hextetCount; i < 8; i++) expanded.push('0')
+  for (let i = left.length; i < parts.length; i++) expanded.push(parts[i])
+  return compressIPv6ZeroRun(expanded)
 }
 
 /**
```

### Fix summary

The patch completely rewrites the IPv6 parsing and normalization logic. It introduces stricter validation for hextets, zone identifiers, and IPv4-in-IPv6 formats. It also correctly handles IPv6 compression and expansion according to RFC 5952 and RFC 6874 for zone identifiers, preventing malformed addresses from being normalized into exploitable forms.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 117 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Go, Python, PHP, RUST, Erlang, Java
> → GHSA-3p68-rc4w-qgx5

## GHSA-fph4-wmhf-6fwf · fast-uri

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `fast-uri` library was vulnerable to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) due to improper handling of percent-encoded hostnames. Specifically, the `unescape` function was applied multiple times to the hostname component, allowing an attacker to bypass hostname validation by double-encoding characters like '%25' (which decodes to '%'). This repeated decoding could transform a seemingly safe hostname into an internal IP address or a different malicious domain after multiple decoding passes.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious URI that, when processed by the vulnerable library, would resolve to an arbitrary internal or external host. This could lead to unauthorized access to internal network resources, metadata services (e.g., cloud metadata APIs), or other sensitive services, potentially exfiltrating data or enabling further attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/index.js
+++ b/index.js
@@ -468,7 +476,7 @@ function parseWithStatus (uri, opts) {
           parsed.scheme = unescape(parsed.scheme)
         }
         if (parsed.host !== undefined) {
-          parsed.host = reescapeHostDelimiters(unescape(parsed.host), isIP)
+          parsed.host = reescapeHostDelimiters(normalizePercentEncoding(parsed.host, true), isIP)
         }
       }
       if (parsed.path) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `malformedHost` status flag to track issues during host canonicalization and ensures that percent-encoding normalization for hostnames is applied only once using `normalizePercentEncoding`. It specifically prevents repeated `unescape` calls on the host component and correctly handles IPv6 zone IDs to avoid re-encoding issues, thus preventing the hostname from being repeatedly decoded and manipulated.

## GHSA-h89q-4j2h-7h88 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-09-02 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an SQL query used in the block search functionality. This query did not properly enforce access controls, allowing it to retrieve content from documents that were marked as hidden or unpublished.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this to access and view the content of documents that were intended to be private or not yet publicly available, leading to unauthorized information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
The provided diff is for a minified JavaScript file (lute.min.js) and does not contain human-readable code changes relevant to the SQL vulnerability. It's a minified build output, making it impossible to extract a meaningful key_diff for the reported SQL issue from this specific patch.
```

### Fix summary

The provided diff is for a minified JavaScript file (lute.min.js) and does not directly show the Go code changes that would address the SQL query vulnerability. Typically, such a fix would involve modifying the SQL query to include proper authorization checks, ensuring that only authorized and published content is returned.

## GHSA-jqff-g426-hqxp · fast-uri

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `fast-uri` library did not properly normalize or validate percent-encoded characters within the URI scheme component. It would unescape the scheme only after initial parsing, allowing an attacker to craft a URI with a percent-encoded scheme (e.g., `h%74tp://example.com`) that would bypass security checks based on the scheme, but then be interpreted as a different scheme (e.g., `http`) by other components or downstream systems.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass security policies that rely on URI scheme validation, potentially leading to SSRF, open redirects, or other vulnerabilities if the URI is used in contexts where scheme-based filtering is critical.

### Key diff

```
--- a/index.js
+++ b/index.js
@@ -420,6 +446,16 @@ function parseWithStatus (uri, opts) {
     parsed.query = matches[7]
     parsed.fragment = matches[8]
 
+    if (parsed.scheme !== undefined) {
+      const decodedScheme = unescape(parsed.scheme)
+      if (VALID_SCHEME.test(decodedScheme)) {
+        parsed.scheme = decodedScheme.toLowerCase()
+      } else {
+        parsed.error = parsed.error || MALFORMED_SCHEME_ERROR
+        malformedScheme = true
+      }
+    }
+
     malformedPercentEncoding = hasMalformedComponentPercentEncoding(matches)
     if (malformedPercentEncoding) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `decodeValidScheme` function to explicitly unescape and validate the scheme component against a regular expression (`VALID_SCHEME`) immediately after parsing and before any further processing or serialization. It also adds a `malformedScheme` status flag to indicate if the scheme is invalid, preventing malformed URIs from being normalized or resolved.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 117 occurrences**

> → GHSA-3p68-rc4w-qgx5

## GHSA-vq4v-j7r6-jq4m · pnpm

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient validation of package names and versions, particularly when using the global virtual store. The system would insert package versions as raw path components, allowing an attacker to use path traversal sequences (e.g., `../../../`) within a tarball dependency's manifest `name` or `version` to escape the intended virtual store directory.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file write or overwrite on the system where `pnpm` is installed by crafting a malicious package manifest. This could lead to remote code execution or other severe system compromise.

### Key diff

```
-    assert!(matches!(error, SymlinkPackageError::InvalidAlias(_)), "got {error:?}");
+    let error = create_symlink_layout(
+        Some(&deps),
+        None,
+        &pkg_name("self"),
+        &skipped,
+        &layout,
+        &virtual_node_modules_dir,
+    )
+    .expect_err("traversal alias must be rejected");
+    assert!(matches!(error, SymlinkPackageError::InvalidAlias(_)), "got {error:?}");
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new validation checks to ensure that virtual store slots and dependency aliases remain contained within their designated directories. Specifically, `validate_virtual_store_slot_containment` checks that no snapshot's computed virtual-store slot escapes the store root, and `create_symlink_layout` now explicitly rejects traversal sequences in dependency aliases.

## GHSA-w878-pj84-3j5v · github.com/axllent/mailpit

2026-09-02 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The SMTP and POP3 command parsers used `bufio.NewReader` without specifying a size, leading to unbounded buffering of command lines. An attacker could send an extremely long command line, causing the server to allocate an arbitrarily large amount of memory to store it before processing or rejecting it.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust the server's memory resources by sending a continuous stream of oversized command lines, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/smtpd/smtpd.go
+++ b/internal/smtpd/smtpd.go
@@ -265,7 +270,7 @@ func (srv *Server) newSession(conn net.Conn) (s *session) {
 	s = &session{
 		srv:  srv,
 		conn: conn,
-		br:   bufio.NewReader(conn),
+		br:   bufio.NewReaderSize(conn, 2048),
 		bw:   bufio.NewWriter(conn),
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `bufio.NewReaderSize` with a fixed buffer size (2048 bytes for SMTP, 512 bytes for POP3) and modifies the `readLine` function to detect and reject oversized lines. If a line exceeds the buffer, the remaining input is drained, and an error indicating 'line too long' is returned to the client, preventing excessive memory allocation.

## GHSA-c59q-g84q-2gj5 · pnpm

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The pnpm package manager, specifically in its handling of the virtual store and lockfile, did not adequately validate dependency names and versions. When using the global virtual store, the version segment of a package key was inserted as a raw path component, allowing path traversal sequences (e.g., `../../../`) to escape the intended virtual store directory. Similarly, dependency aliases could contain path traversal sequences, leading to symlinks being created outside the expected `node_modules` directory.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious `lockfile` or dependency alias that, when processed by pnpm, would cause symlinks to be created outside the virtual store or `node_modules` directory. This could lead to arbitrary file creation or overwriting, potentially allowing for remote code execution or other system compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pacquet/crates/package-manager/src/validate_lockfile_paths.rs
+++ b/pacquet/crates/package-manager/src/validate_lockfile_paths.rs
@@ -24,6 +24,9 @@
     let mut escaped: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
     for key in snapshots.keys() {
         // Lexical containment: the slot does not exist yet, so this must
-        // not touch the filesystem.
         if !is_subdir(layout.package_store_dir(), &layout.slot_dir(key)) {
             escaped.insert(key.to_string());
         }
```
```diff
--- a/pacquet/crates/package-manager/src/create_symlink_layout/tests.rs
+++ b/pacquet/crates/package-manager/src/create_symlink_layout/tests.rs
@@ -259,3 +259,25 @@
     let error = create_symlink_layout(
         Some(&deps),
         None,
-        &pkg_name("self"),
-        &skipped,
-        &layout,
-        &virtual_node_modules_dir,
-    )
-    .expect_err("traversal alias must be rejected");
-    assert!(matches!(error, SymlinkPackageError::InvalidAlias(_)), "got {error:?}");
+
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces two main validation checks. First, `validate_virtual_store_slot_containment` ensures that all computed virtual-store slots resolve within the store root, preventing version-based path traversals. Second, `create_symlink_layout` now explicitly rejects dependency aliases that contain path traversal sequences, preventing symlinks from being created outside the intended `node_modules` directory.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 56 occurrences**

## GHSA-cxq5-97v7-87j8 · orval

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The 'orval' package, used for OpenAPI spec generation, did not properly restrict external references ($ref) within OpenAPI documents. When processing an OpenAPI spec, it would resolve any $ref without validating if the target URL or file path was allowed, leading to uncontrolled access to external resources or local files.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious OpenAPI specification containing arbitrary $ref values. This would allow them to perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) to access internal network resources or cloud metadata, or achieve local/remote file inclusion to read sensitive files from the system where 'orval' is run.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/orval/src/import-specs.ts
+++ b/packages/orval/src/import-specs.ts
@@ -41,9 +41,45 @@ async function resolveSpec(
     unsafeDisableValidation = false,
   }: ResolveSpecOptions,
 ): Promise<OpenApiDocument> {
-  const dereferencedData = await bundleAndDereferenceExternalRefs(
+  const allowedRefs = parserOptions?.externalRefs?.allow ?? [];
+  const isWildcard = allowedRefs.includes('*');
+
+  // Load the top-level spec so we can scan for external $refs before
+  // bundle() resolves them. The top-level target is trusted (user-configured
+  // input.target); only the $ref values inside the spec are untrusted.
+  const { data: specData, origin } = await loadSpec(
     input,
+    parserOptions?.headers,
+  );
+
+  // Enforce the allow-list on refs found in the top-level spec.
+  // Transitive refs (inside external docs) are enforced by the loader wrappers.
+  if (!isWildcard) {
+    const refs = collectExternalRefs(specData);
+    const disallowed = refs.filter(
+      (ref) => !isAllowedRef(ref, allowedRefs, origin),
+    );
+    if (disallowed.length > 0) {
+      throw new Error(formatDisallowedRefsError(disallowed, allowedRefs));
+    }
+  } else {
+    const docs = [
+      ...new Set(collectExternalRefs(specData).map(getRefDocument)),
+    ];
+    if (docs.length > 0) {
+      logWarning(
+        `External $ref documents being resolved:\n` +
+          docs.map((d) => `  - ${d}`).join('\n'),
+      );
+    }
+  }
+
+  const dereferencedData = await bundleAndDereferenceExternalRefs(
+    specData,
     parserOptions,
+    origin,
+    isWildcard,
+    allowedRefs,
   );
 
   // Apply user-provided transformer before validation so users can repair
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an allow-list mechanism for external $ref documents. It first loads the top-level spec to collect and validate all external $refs against the user-configured allow-list. New loader wrappers (`createSafeFileLoader`, `createSafeUrlLoader`) are implemented to enforce these restrictions during the bundling and dereferencing process, preventing unauthorized access.

## GHSA-8mgp-746c-j5xp · nltk

2026-09-02 · Python · **HIGH** 7.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The NLTK library's model-artifact APIs, specifically `Maxent_NE_Chunker.save_params` and `build_model`, previously used guessable paths in shared, world-writable temporary directories (e.g., `/tmp/english_ace_<fmt>/` or `/tmp/ne_chunker_<fmt>.pickle`). This allowed an attacker to pre-create or symlink these paths, redirecting file writes outside the intended NLTK data sandbox or reading sensitive data.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary file write by symlinking the predictable temporary file paths to locations outside the NLTK data root, potentially leading to remote code execution or denial of service. They could also read sensitive files if the symlink pointed to a readable file.

### Key diff

```
--- a/nltk/chunk/named_entity.py
+++ b/nltk/chunk/named_entity.py
@@ -340,17 +343,51 @@ def load_params(self):
         )
         self._tagger = NEChunkParserTagger(classifier=mc)
 
-    def save_params(self):
-        from nltk.classify.maxent import save_maxent_params
+    @property
+    def save_dir(self) -> str:
+        if self._save_dir is None:
+            self._save_dir = make_staging_dir(prefix=f"nltk_ne_chunker_{self._fmt}_")
+        return self._save_dir
+
+    def save_params(self, tab_dir: str | None = None) -> str:
         classif = self._tagger._classifier
         ecg = classif._encoding
         wgt = classif._weights
         mpg = ecg._mapping
         lab = ecg._labels
         aon = ecg._alwayson
-        fmt = self._fmt
-        save_maxent_params(wgt, mpg, lab, aon, tab_dir=f"/tmp/english_ace_{fmt}/")
+        if tab_dir is None:
+            tab_dir = self.save_dir
+        validate_path(tab_dir, context="Maxent_NE_Chunker.save_params")
+
+        save_maxent_params(wgt, mpg, lab, aon, tab_dir=tab_dir)
+        return tab_dir
 
 
 def build_model(fmt="multiclass"):
@@ -394,10 +431,11 @@ def build_model(fmt="binary"):
             cmp_chunks(correct, guess)
     print(chunkscore)
 
-    outfilename = f"/tmp/ne_chunker_{fmt}.pickle"
+    outdir = make_staging_dir(prefix=f"nltk_ne_chunker_{fmt}_")
+    outfilename = f"{outdir}/ne_chunker_{fmt}.pickle"
     print(f"Saving chunker to {outfilename}...")
 
-    with open(outfilename, "wb") as outfile:
+    with pathsec_open(outfilename, "wb", context="build_model") as outfile:
         pickle.dump(cp, outfile, -1)
 
     return cp
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `make_staging_dir` to create private, unpredictably named, and mode 0700 directories for saving model artifacts. It also adds `validate_path` calls to explicitly check any user-provided `tab_dir` against the NLTK data sandbox, preventing writes to arbitrary external paths.

## GHSA-pp4x-ccxq-6r33 · sulu/sulu

2026-09-02 · PHP · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application allowed users to upload media files and control their MIME type. When these files were served, the Content-Disposition header was set to 'inline' by default for certain MIME types, including potentially dangerous ones like 'text/html' or 'application/xml'. This allowed an attacker to upload a malicious file (e.g., an HTML file containing JavaScript) and have it rendered directly in the browser within the application's origin, leading to Stored XSS.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript in the context of the user's browser, potentially leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
-        $response->headers->set('Content-Type', !empty($mimeType) ? $mimeType : 'application/octet-stream');
-        $response->headers->set('Content-Disposition', $disposition);
-        $response->headers->set('Content-length', $fileSize);
+        if ($dangerousInlineMimeType && ResponseHeaderBag::DISPOSITION_INLINE === $dispositionType) {
+            $dispositionType = ResponseHeaderBag::DISPOSITION_ATTACHMENT;
+        }
+        // ... (other headers)
+        $response->headers->set('Content-Security-Policy', 'sandbox');
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a list of 'dangerous' inline MIME types (e.g., text/html, application/xml). If a file with one of these MIME types is requested with an 'inline' disposition, the disposition is forced to 'attachment' instead, prompting a download rather than in-browser rendering. Additionally, a 'Content-Security-Policy: sandbox' header is added to further mitigate potential risks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 107 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, PHP, Java, Go, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-3gqm-fcw5-w839 · nltk

2026-09-02 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incomplete Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) protection mechanism. Specifically, the `validate_network_url` function's check for forbidden IP addresses was insufficient, allowing certain non-global IP addresses (like those used in carrier-grade NAT) to bypass the filter. Additionally, the original `urlopen` implementation was susceptible to DNS rebinding attacks because it would re-resolve hostnames at connection time, potentially allowing an attacker to switch a validated public IP to a forbidden internal IP.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the existing SSRF protection to access internal network resources or services that were intended to be inaccessible from the public internet. This could lead to information disclosure, unauthorized access to internal systems, or further attacks within the internal network.

### Key diff

```
--- a/nltk/pathsec.py
+++ b/nltk/pathsec.py
@@ -244,7 +274,7 @@ def validate_network_url(url_input, context="NetworkIO"):
 
         for result in _resolve_hostname(parsed.hostname or ""):
             ip = ipaddress.ip_address(result[4][0])
-            if ip.is_loopback or ip.is_link_local or ip.is_multicast or ip.is_private:
+            if _ip_is_forbidden(ip):
                 msg = f"Security Violation [{context}]: SSRF attempt to restricted IP {ip}"
                 if ENFORCE:
                     raise PermissionError(msg)
@@ -279,17 +411,41 @@ def urlopen(url, *args, **kwargs):
 
     # Safely inherit proxy settings without reusing handler instances
     # (Reusing instances overwrites their .parent, breaking the global opener)
+    proxied = False
+    has_proxy_handler = False
     if urllib.request._opener is not None:
         for handler in urllib.request._opener.handlers:
             if isinstance(handler, urllib.request.ProxyHandler):
+                has_proxy_handler = True
                 # Copy the dictionary to prevent shared mutable state
                 isolated_proxies = dict(handler.proxies) if handler.proxies
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a more robust IP address validation function, `_ip_is_forbidden`, which strictly allows only globally routable addresses and explicitly forbids all other categories (loopback, private, link-local, multicast, etc.). It also implements `_pinned_connection` and custom `_SafeHTTPConnection`/`_SafeHTTPSConnection` classes to ensure that hostnames are resolved and validated only once, and connections are made to the *pinned* numeric IP addresses, thereby preventing DNS rebinding attacks.

## GHSA-6gmq-8vp8-gcm6 · xmldom

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The `xmldom` library did not properly validate the `nodeName` of an `EntityReference` node when `requireWellFormed: true` was enabled during serialization. An attacker could create an `EntityReference` with an invalid XML name, which would then be serialized verbatim, leading to XML fragment injection.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary XML fragments into the serialized output, potentially leading to malformed XML documents, denial of service, or other downstream processing issues if the consuming application does not handle malformed XML robustly.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/dom.js
+++ b/lib/dom.js
@@ -2456,19 +2456,29 @@ Document.prototype = {
 	createEntityReference: function (name) {
-		if (!g.Name.test(name)) {
+		if (!g.Name_exact.test(name)) {
 			throw new DOMException(DOMException.INVALID_CHARACTER_ERR, 'not a valid xml name "' + name + '"');
 		}
 		if (this.type === 'html') {
@@ -3335,6 +3361,12 @@ function serializeToString(node, buf, visibleNamespaces, opts) {
 						buf.push('<?', n.target, ' ', n.data, '?>');
 						return null;
 					case ENTITY_REFERENCE_NODE:
+						if (requireWellFormed && !g.Name_exact.test(n.nodeName)) {
+							throw new DOMException(
+								'The entity reference name "' + n.nodeName + '" is not a valid XML Name',
+								DOMExceptionName.InvalidStateError
+							);
+						}
 						buf.push('&', n.nodeName, ';');
 						return null;
 					//case ENTITY_NODE:
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a stricter validation for `EntityReference.nodeName` during `Document.createEntityReference` using `g.Name_exact.test(name)`. Additionally, during serialization with `requireWellFormed: true`, it now re-validates `nodeName` against the XML `Name` production and throws an `InvalidStateError` if it's invalid, preventing the injection of malformed entity references.

## GHSA-6mr6-jvcr-2f25 · orval

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient sanitization of user-controlled input, specifically schema property names and default values, when generating Zod validation schemas and route paths. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary JavaScript code into computed property keys or template literals, leading to import-time RCE.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the system generating the client code, potentially compromising the build process or development environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts
@@ -816,15 +834,16 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
       // `.default()` rejects against its mutable parameter type (#3399).
       const entries = Object.entries(schema.default)
         .map(([key, value]) => {
+          const safeKey = JSON.stringify(key);
           if (isString(value)) {
-            return `${key}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} as const`;
+            return `${safeKey}: ${JSON.stringify(value)} as const`;
           }
 
           if (Array.isArray(value)) {
             const arrayItems = value.map((item) =>
               isString(item) ? `${JSON.stringify(item)} as const` : `${item}`,
             );
-            return `${key}: [${arrayItems.join(', ')}]`;
+            return `${safeKey}: [${arrayItems.join(', ')}]`;
           }
 
           if (
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape all user-controlled strings that are embedded into generated JavaScript code, particularly for object keys, default values, and route path segments. This prevents injection of malicious code by ensuring that input is treated as literal string data rather than executable code.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 13 occurrences**
> Languages: Java, PHP, Python, JavaScript
> → GHSA-xj4f-8jjg-vx4q
> → GHSA-x7m9-mwc2-g6w2
> → GHSA-gg2g-p7xc-qqmm
> → GHSA-65p8-9433-jpcp
> → GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6
> → GHSA-9473-5f9j-94wq
> → GHSA-f5wm-88jv-g5hx
> → GHSA-8cfw-pcwh-v63w
> → GHSA-hrwp-4hh9-c8r8
> → GHSA-73mf-m39p-wpm9
> → GHSA-jw39-3688-r4rx
> → GHSA-p4cg-3328-rvfg

🔗 [View commit](https://github.com/orval-labs/orval/commit/8ef1bfdf3f9bcaf9dabfbe2e42887f1c0e159ab6)

## GHSA-fj2p-qj2f-74v5 · getgrav/grav

2026-09-02 · PHP · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `Blueprint::dynamicData()` method used `call_user_func_array()` to execute dynamic callables specified in blueprint definitions. An attacker could inject a malicious callable (e.g., `system` or `exec`) and its arguments through user-controlled sources like form blueprints assembled from page frontmatter, leading to arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary PHP code on the server, potentially leading to full system compromise, data theft, or defacement.

### Key diff

```
--- a/system/src/Grav/Common/Data/Blueprint.php
+++ b/system/src/Grav/Common/Data/Blueprint.php
@@ -434,6 +435,20 @@ protected function dynamicData(array &$field, $property, array &$call)
             $params = [];
         }
 
+        // Security guard. A `data-*@` directive may come from a user-controlled
+        // source, most notably a form blueprint the Form plugin assembles from
+        // page frontmatter, and this method hands the callable and its arguments
+        // straight to call_user_func_array(). Refuse anything that could run
+        // arbitrary code: a directly dangerous top-level function (system, exec,
+        // ...), or any argument that is itself a dangerous callable. The latter
+        // is what stops a benign-looking helper from being used as a trampoline,
+        // e.g. Utils::arrayFilterRecursive($cmd, 'system'). Legitimate providers
+        // are static methods returning option arrays and never take a callable
+        // argument, so real blueprints are unaffected. (GHSA-fj2p-qj2f-74v5)
+        if (!$this->isSafeDynamicCall($function, $params)) {
+            return;
+        }
+
         [$o, $f] = explode('::', (string) $function, 2);
 
         $data = null;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a security guard `isSafeDynamicCall()` in `Blueprint::dynamicData()` that checks if the callable function or any of its arguments are dangerous. It prevents direct execution of known dangerous functions and recursively scans arguments to block 'trampoline' attacks where a benign helper function is used to invoke a dangerous callable.

## GHSA-p3m8-78j2-g5p3 · nltk

2026-09-02 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The NLTK library had its security enforcement toggle (ENFORCE) set to False by default, effectively disabling all path security controls. Additionally, the `validate_network_url` function did not properly handle `file://` URIs, allowing non-local authorities and incorrect path conversions on Windows, which could lead to path traversal.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass security checks, potentially leading to arbitrary file access (read/write/delete) or execution of malicious code by manipulating file paths or network URLs used by NLTK's downloader or other components.

### Key diff

```
--- a/nltk/pathsec.py
+++ b/nltk/pathsec.py
@@ -21,7 +21,8 @@
 from urllib.parse import unquote, urlparse
 
 # Security Enforcement Toggle
-ENFORCE = False
+# ENFORCE = False
+ENFORCE = True
 
 _ALLOWED_ROOTS_CACHE = None
 _LAST_DATA_PATHS = None
```

### Fix summary

The patch sets the `ENFORCE` flag to `True` by default, activating security controls. It also enhances `validate_network_url` to explicitly reject non-local `file://` URI authorities and correctly convert Windows `file://` paths before validation. Furthermore, the downloader's locking mechanism was refactored to prevent race conditions during package installation.

## GHSA-p4cg-3328-rvfg · orval

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→TEMPLATE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from improper sanitization of user-controlled input, specifically default values for query parameters and OpenAPI path segments. These values were directly embedded into generated TypeScript template literals without sufficient escaping, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary code that would be executed at import time when the generated code was loaded.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution on the system generating or consuming the `orval` output, potentially leading to full system compromise or data exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/zod/src/index.ts
+++ b/packages/zod/src/index.ts
@@ -852,9 +870,7 @@ export const generateZodValidationSchemaDefinition = (
       defaultValue = entries.length === 0 ? `{}` : `{ ${entries} }`;
     } else {
       // OpenApiSchemaObject defines default as 'any'
-      const rawStringified = stringify(schema.default);
-      defaultValue =
-        rawStringified === undefined
-          ? 'null'
-          : rawStringified.replaceAll("'", '`');
+      defaultValue = formatDefaultValue(schema.default);
 
       // If the schema is an array with enum items, inject inplace to avoid issues with default values
       const isArrayWithEnumItems =
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces the `jsesc` library to properly escape string values before they are embedded into template literals. It also ensures that object keys and array items are correctly stringified and escaped, preventing injection of malicious code into the generated TypeScript files.

## GHSA-vmg4-6gfg-83qx · apostrophe

2026-09-02 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from insufficient validation in the `apos.util.get` and `apos.util.set` methods, which allowed user-supplied paths to traverse the prototype chain. While some dangerous segments like `__proto__` were explicitly blocked as the final segment, the system did not prevent traversing inherited properties in intermediate path segments, enabling attackers to modify shared `Object.prototype` properties.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve a persistent Denial of Service (DoS) by polluting global `Object.prototype` properties. For example, modifying `Object.prototype.toString.call` could break core JavaScript functionality across the entire application, leading to a server crash or malfunction until the process is restarted.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/util/index.js
+++ b/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/util/index.js
@@ -762,7 +783,7 @@ module.exports = {
             if (o == null) {
               return undefined;
             }
-            if (unsafePathSegments.has(p)) {
+            if (unsafePathSegments.has(p) || !ownProperty(o, p)) {
               // Never read through the prototype chain (CWE-1321)
               return undefined;
             }
@@ -838,6 +859,15 @@ module.exports = {
         } else {
           o = obj;
         }
+        for (i = 0; (i < (path.length - 1)); i++) {
+          p = path[i];
+          if (!ownProperty(o, p)) {
+            throw self.apos.error(
+              'invalid',
+              `No own property "${p}" to traverse in dot path`
+            );
+          }
+          o = o[p];
+        }
         p = path[i];
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an `ownProperty` helper function to check if a property belongs directly to an object, rather than being inherited. This function is then used in `apos.util.get` and `apos.util.set` to ensure that only own properties are traversed or written to, preventing access to and modification of prototype chain properties.
